## 12. Chat memory commands (MD-13, slice L9)

> **Owner, 2026-09-24 (MD-13):** add direct chat commands `/remember`, `/forget` and `/memories`, handled by the daemon with **no model involved**, like `/status` and `/tasks`. Because the human typed them, `/remember` saves with origin **`user`** — the in-chat equivalent of the Memory view. Offered only on attended app/CLI turns. No memory *skill* (SKILL.md) is added.

**Why this is G2-safe.** G2 forbids a *model* tool call from minting origin `user`. These commands never reach a model: the deterministic tier (`orchestrator/intent/mod.rs`, parsed before skills and before the main loop; `orchestrator/handlers.rs`) executes them and answers with a fixed template. The text that becomes the note is exactly what the human typed on an attended turn. The user-directed surfaces of 03 §4 therefore gain one member — *a deterministic memory command typed on an attended app or CLI chat turn* — and 03 §7a's `MemoryAccessContext` table gains that row with `surface: UserSurface`.

### 12.1 The commands

| Command | Behaviour | Reply (deterministic) |
|---|---|---|
| `/remember [--kind <k>] [--global \| --project] <text>` | Stores a note through `MemoryService` with `surface: UserSurface`, origin `user`. `kind` defaults to `fact` (the six kinds of §4 are accepted); the scope defaults by kind (03 §3 rule 2) against the request's marker-found project root; `--global` / `--project` override (`--project` with no project root is `invalid_input`, §7b P1). Title: the first line, cut at 80 characters on a char boundary; body: the whole text. Exact dedup answers the existing id (§7b P8). | `Saved as a note you wrote: “<title>” (<id8>, <scope>). It is used from your next message.` — or the refusal's own sentence. |
| `/forget <id-or-prefix>` | Resolves a **unique** id prefix (at least 8 hex characters) among the notes this request can read; fetches the current revision; forgets it (tombstone-first, §6). An ambiguous prefix lists the matches and forgets nothing. | `Forgot “<title>” (<id8>).` followed by the service's retained-copies sentence. |
| `/forget <text>` (not an id-shaped token) | Runs `search` (limit 5, active notes) and **forgets nothing**. | The matches as `<id8>  <title>  [<kind>/<scope>]` lines, then `Send /forget <id> to forget one.` — or `Nothing matched “<text>”.` |
| `/memories` | The 10 most recently updated active notes this request can read, and the number of pending suggestions (L3). | One line per note as above; `N suggestions are waiting in the Memory view.` when N > 0. |
| `/memories <query>` | `search` (limit 8, active notes). | One line per hit with its match reason; `Nothing matched “<query>”.` |

Output is bounded (at most 10 lines of notes, each line at most 160 characters). No note body is printed beyond the title; `/memories` is a pointer, not a reader (`memory_read` and the Memory view read).

### 12.2 Where they run, and where they refuse

- **Attended app/CLI turns only** (`unattended == false`, lane source `gui` or `cli`). A connector turn (Telegram, Discord, iMessage), an unattended app or CLI turn (a piped `openalpaca chat`), a scheduled skill, a follow-up and `/v1/command process` get: ``Memory commands work only in the OpenAlpaca app's chat, or in `openalpaca chat` run at a terminal — not with its input or output piped.`` — deterministic, nothing written (rev 3.22, residue R5: the first wording, "…work in the OpenAlpaca app and the CLI.", contradicted itself to a piped CLI; `orchestrator/memory_commands.rs`, `NOT_HERE`). (Same boundary as `memory_confirm`, §4.3; the iMessage non-owner defect is why connectors are excluded.)
- **No owner** (`Principal::System`) or **no memory service**: `Memory is not available here.`
- **Attachments** on a memory-command turn are skipped with a reason (`skip_turn_attachments`, as the other deterministic arms do).
- **Learning** never reads these rows: the transcript window already leaves out user rows starting with `/` together with their answer (§1.2).

### 12.3 Parsing and reserved names

- `IntentParser` gains `Intent::MemoryCommand { verb: Remember | Forget | Memories, args: String }`, parsed before skill matching — on the production path (`parse_with_skills_and_router`) before task control too, which the three words cannot collide with (rev 3.22: this line said "after task control"); the command word is case-insensitive and must be followed by whitespace or end of input (`/remembering` is not a command). The args are kept **verbatim** (not lower-cased).
- `/remember`, `/forget` and `/memories` become **reserved slash names**: a skill whose name is one of them is not reachable by slash (the skill loader logs one WARN naming it at load), and `create-skill` refuses those names.
- `intent_type()` gains `"memory_command"` for telemetry.

### 12.4 Plan row L9

| Slice | What lands | Depends on | Size |
|---|---|---|---|
| **L9** | `Intent::MemoryCommand` and its parser; `orchestrator/memory_commands.rs` (the three handlers over `MemoryService` with a `UserSurface` context built from the attended turn); the refusals of §12.2; reserved names in the skill loader and `create-skill`; `memory_changed` published by the service as for any write; docs | L3 (suggestion count), L5 (none) | S–M |

**Files:** `crates/openalpaca_core/src/orchestrator/intent/{mod.rs,tests.rs}`, `orchestrator/handlers.rs`, `orchestrator/memory_commands.rs` (new) + tests, `memory/service/context.rs` (the new constructor), the skill loader and `config/skills/create-skill/`, `docs/CLI_Manual.md` (chat commands), `docs/GUI_Manual.md`, `docs/Daemon_Manual.md` (the deterministic tier), 03 §4/§7a (the new user-directed surface and table row).

**Evidence (tests first):**
- the parse table: each verb, case, verbatim args, `/remembering` not a command, a skill named `remember` not reached by slash;
- `/remember …` on an attended GUI turn writes one note with origin `user` and `surface: UserSurface`, **with zero model calls** (a scripted provider that fails the test if called);
- `--kind`, `--global`, `--project`, and `--project` with no root refused `invalid_input`;
- the same command on a connector turn, an unattended turn and a `System`-principal turn writes nothing and answers the refusal sentence;
- `/forget <id8>` forgets exactly that note (tombstone present, file gone); `/forget <ambiguous prefix>` and `/forget <text>` forget nothing and list matches;
- `/memories` and `/memories <query>` are bounded and print no body;
- one `memory_changed` per write; the rows are not in the next learning window.

## 13. Layered retrieval, index-first recall and conversation summaries (MD-14…MD-16, slice L10)

> **Owner, 2026-09-24:** adopt three ideas from claude-mem's `mem-search` / `how-it-works` skills (`github.com/thedotmack/claude-mem`, `plugin/skills/mem-search/SKILL.md`, `plugin/skills/how-it-works/SKILL.md`, read 2026-09-24): **MD-14** layered memory tools; **MD-15** index-first recall each turn, measured against excerpt recall; **MD-16** conversation summaries as `episode` notes, findable but never injected automatically. The reference's rule is "filter first, fetch later": a search returns a compact index (about 50–100 tokens a result), a timeline shows what surrounds an entry in time, and full details are fetched only for chosen ids, in one batch — the reference reports about 10× fewer tokens.

### 13.1 Layered memory tools (MD-14, L10a)

All three stay under the `memory_read` capability (no new grant).

| Tool | Change |
|---|---|
| `memory_search` | New `detail: "index" \| "snippets"`, **default `"index"`**. An index row is `{id, date, kind, scope, status, title, reason, size}` — `date` is `observed_at` when present, else `created_at`; `reason` is the match reason; `size` the note's approximate token count (bytes ÷ 4) so the model can budget a read. `"snippets"` is today's output. New `date_from`, `date_to` (inclusive, on `date`) and `order_by: "relevance" \| "newest" \| "oldest"` (default `relevance`). `limit` default 10, max 50. The filters of §5/§7b are unchanged. |
| `memory_read` | Takes `ids: [..]` (1–10) instead of one `id`; one call returns every note, each with its revision and provenance, inside the unchanged 32 KiB total (split evenly; a cut note says so). A single id is `ids: [id]` — the app is unreleased, so the old argument breaks cleanly (03 §7). Each id is re-checked for access as today. |
| `memory_timeline` (new) | Input: exactly one of `anchor` (a note id), `query` (the first search hit becomes the anchor) or `date` (an instant); `before` and `after` (default 5, max 20). Returns index rows (the `memory_search` index shape) in time order around the anchor, **including** superseded, archived and ended-window notes marked by `status`, and `episode` notes (§13.3), so "what did I decide before the June move" and "what did we discuss last week" have one tool. Candidates and suggestions are excluded. |

The three tool descriptions teach the layered workflow in two sentences each ("Search returns an index; pick ids; read only those — several at once"). The main loop's memory guidance in the system prompt says the same in one line. API and CLI parity: `GET /v1/memories/search` accepts `detail`, `date_from`, `date_to`, `order_by`; `GET /v1/memories/timeline` is new; `openalpaca memory search --since --until --order` and `openalpaca memory timeline <id|--query q|--date d>` are new. The GUI keeps its current calls.

### 13.2 Index-first recall each turn (MD-15, L10b)

A new key `[orchestrator.memory] recall_mode = "index" | "excerpts"`, **default `"index"`** (owner-adopted; §13.4 measures it against `"excerpts"`, and a regression is reported to the owner, not silently reverted).

- `"excerpts"` is today's §5 delivery (up to 8 chunks, 8 KiB).
- `"index"` delivers, inside the same 8 KiB: the pinned notes (unchanged, 2 KiB sub-budget); the **top 2** recalled notes as excerpts; then an **index block** of up to 8 further relevant notes, one line each — `<id8> <date> <kind> “<title>” — <reason>` — under the line `More notes that may be relevant (read with memory_read):`. All of it stays quoted reference data (03 §5 step 8).
- `memory_used` gains `reason: "index"` for a listed note; the recently-used ring records it as listed, not delivered.

### 13.3 Conversation summaries as `episode` notes (MD-16, L10c)

- **When:** at the end of a conversation's quiet period of **at least 30 minutes**, on a conversation switch, and in the shutdown drain — the MD-10 triggers minus the 120-second idle one, which is too frequent for a summary — and only when at least **4 user turns** have accumulated since the session's last episode. The window, byte budget, cost gates and yielding to chat of §1 apply; one call per episode.
- **What:** one note, `kind: episode`, title `Conversation on <YYYY-MM-DD>: <topic>`, body: what was discussed, what was decided, what was left open, in at most 1 KiB of prose; `observed_at` = the last turn's time; `sources` = the session's message rows in the window; no `fact_key`, no aliases required, no `supersedes`.
- **Authority and visibility:** origin `extracted_other` (the model's own wording, lowest rank, so it can supersede nothing). Unlike a candidate, an episode is `active` so that `memory_search`, `memory_timeline`, `memory_list` and the Memory view find it — but **automatic recall excludes `kind = episode`** (per-turn recall and the index block alike), so it is never injected on its own. This is a stated exception to MD-2's "assistant-derived stays a candidate", justified by the owner's choice: an episode is a record of a conversation, not a claim about the user, and it never enters a prompt unless the model or the user asks for it.
- **Forgetting:** `/forget`, the Memory view and `memory_forget` work as for any note. Deleting or clearing a conversation (LQ4) also **forgets the episodes whose sources all lie in that session**.
- **Cost:** counted under the same durable daily gates as extraction (§1.8), with its own per-day count cap `episode_max_per_day` (default 100 since LQ21, rev 3.51; 20 before).

### 13.4 Plan rows L10a–L10c

| Slice | What lands | Depends on | Size |
|---|---|---|---|
| **L10a** | `memory_search` index/snippets, date filters, `order_by`; batch `memory_read`; `memory_timeline`; the descriptions and the one-line prompt guidance; route and CLI parity | L5 | M |
| **L10b** | `recall_mode` with the index block; `memory_used` reason `index`; the config key with its registry row, `daemon_config_cli` row and `config/daemon.toml` line | L10a | S |
| **L10c** | Episode writing on the three triggers; the recall exclusion; the conversation-delete forget; `episode_max_per_day` | L1, L10a | M |

**Evidence (tests first):**
- L10a: an index row carries no body and every field above; `date_from`/`date_to` bound on `observed_at` else `created_at`; `order_by` newest/oldest; a 10-id `memory_read` returns all within 32 KiB and refuses an 11th id; a foreign id in the batch is refused without leaking the others' order; `memory_timeline` by anchor, by query and by date returns neighbours in time order with superseded/ended notes marked and candidates absent; the route and CLI mirror the tool.
- L10b: `recall_mode = "excerpts"` reproduces today's delivery byte for byte (a golden test); `"index"` delivers pinned + 2 excerpts + at most 8 index lines inside 8 KiB; an index note appears in `memory_used` with reason `index`.
- L10c: no episode before 4 user turns; one episode after a 30-minute quiet period, a switch, or the shutdown drain (the harness's deterministic flush drives all three); an episode is returned by search and timeline and **never** by per-turn recall; deleting the conversation forgets it; the daily cap holds.
- L8 gains: the D run in both recall modes (`D-excerpts`, `D-index`), reporting accuracy, input tokens per turn and tool rounds; and a small **episode question split** (`scripts/eval/memory/questions-episodes.json`, about 20 "what did we discuss / decide on …" questions over the learning corpus), answered after a learning run.

  - **A failing suggestion ended the forget before the suggestions named after it** (guarantees nit 1, probe P3). *Fixed in the code, the review's second option* (`2b49639e`): rev 3.43's forget took the named pending suggestions first but returned at the first one whose own forget failed — its read succeeds; its partition is quarantined, or its file cannot be removed — so `[failing suggestion, suggestion]` left the second a candidate, its turn's row unsuppressed, where `[suggestion, failing suggestion]` forgot it; `MemoryService::forget`'s doc and 03 §6 said the result did not depend on where it stood. Every named suggestion now has its turn and the first failure is returned after them, before the other targets run; the withdrawals the landed forgets owe still run first, and each target is still read once. **Why the code, not the claim**: the change is small — the first pass keeps its first error until the pass ends instead of returning it — each suggestion's turn is its own single-note mutation under its own writer slot, exactly what a retry of that suggestion alone would do — and it gives the guarantee 03 §6 states. Test: `a_suggestion_whose_forget_fails_leaves_every_other_named_suggestion_its_turn` (a quarantined project partition, and a read-only project `notes/`; both orders), red on the code before in both variants. The route's doc and `forget_notes`' say so, and the CLI Manual's account of a refused request.
  - **The deferral of a target the forget cannot read was unpinned** (tests nit 1). *Pinned* (`3f3a2307`): `b2b877d2` reads every target first and defers a failed read so the target fails in its own turn; the one error-path test quarantines a partition, which still reads. `a_target_that_cannot_be_read_fails_in_its_own_turn` (the reviewer's probe: the project's `notes/` unlistable; `[note, unreadable, suggestion]` and `[suggestion, note, unreadable]`) — the suggestion and the note are forgotten in both; red with the read's error returned at once.
  - **03 §7a's CLI syntax said `memory forget` sends "DELETE each"** (consistency 1, minor). It has sent `POST /v1/memories/forget` in requests of up to 20 since rev 3.41 (A3), and the pending suggestions first since rev 3.42; the line now says so, and `memory suggestions`' "one line each" gains the `changes:` line A4 added — both marked in place, with rev 7.12's list of §7a amendments corrected (03 rev 7.15).
  - **The Daemon Manual did not say the daemon forgets the named pending suggestions first** (consistency nit 2): its `POST /v1/memories/forget` row and the tools paragraph now say it — the named pending suggestions first, every one of them even after one fails, then the other targets in the order given, the results in the order given.
  - **The manuals said a candidate's forget spares "messages"** (consistency nit 3): a run's candidates cite the run's `task:` row, which is the case rev 3.43 fixed. The Daemon and CLI Manuals now say the messages or runs, and the Daemon Manual's episode paragraph drops its "only".
  - **The status line said L8 "is running from a pinned worktree"** (consistency nit 4): the first L8 runs are done (their results go into `10-learning-evaluation.md`, not written yet), the slices that followed them have landed, and the re-measurement on the final build is running.
  - **Rev 3.43's entry labelled the shield's documentation finding "consistency nits 7 and 8"** (consistency nit 5): round 2's consistency review numbers them 3 and 4; corrected in place, marked.
  - **Evidence** (session scratchpad, `memeval/l8/records/nits.md` and `records/nits/`: the red, green and negative-control logs).
- **rev 3.43 (2026-09-27), the L8 close-out's repair round 2 (`91fe6bef..b2b877d2` and the documentation commit that records this entry).** The close-out's second round of three reviews — tests, guarantees, consistency — as the lead listed them for repair: 3 minor findings and 6 nits. Each is fixed; none is rejected. Behavioural fixes are tested first, each new test red on the code before its fix. §3.3, §10's LQ11 and LQ17 rows and the status line are amended in place, and one clause of rev 3.42's entry is corrected in place, marked; 03 is rev 7.14. No route, event, field, migration or index schema changes.
  - **The shield covered a pending suggestion only** (guarantees 1, a residual of A2 that predates the close-out). *Fixed, the review's first option* (`d9840624`): every candidate of a run cites the run's `task:` row (`task_extraction/mod.rs`), and learning's inferred candidates from one turn cite that turn's row; the person confirmed one and rejected another with Forget — the only reject the Memory view offers a candidate that names no note — or the model forgot it with `memory_forget` under a card naming that candidate alone, and the forget suppressed the shared row, so the note the person had just confirmed left automatic recall with no word about it (and only if the person confirmed before rejecting). The forget of every pending candidate now suppresses none of the sources of the note it names (whatever its origin) or of any note of saved origin in the partition; its other sources are suppressed as before. **Why not a withdrawing reject in the Memory view**: the model's `memory_forget` of such a candidate would still retire the confirmed sibling. The reviewer's negative control showed no existing test or file-level fixture depends on the narrower shield, and this round's gates agree. Tests: storage `a_candidates_forget_suppresses_no_row_a_saved_note_cites` (a run's two candidates, one confirmed; a row only the other cites still suppressed) and service `forgetting_a_candidate_leaves_a_sibling_the_user_confirmed` (the review's probe P1, its four variants: a run's row and a turn's row, by the tool and by the person), both red before.
  - **A failed forget withdrew a suggestion it named after the failure** (guarantees 2, through rev 3.42's `d515613f`). *Fixed, the review's first option* (`b2b877d2`): `[note, failing, suggestion]` forgot the note, failed, then withdrew the suggestion with the note's other suggestions — its rows never suppressed, a retry answered `not_found` — where `[suggestion, note, failing]` forgot it. The service now takes the named pending suggestions first and the other targets after them in the order given, the rule `openalpaca memory forget` already followed, so the tool and `POST /v1/memories/forget` follow it too; the results keep the order given. Each target is still read once — the read that places it is the one its turn uses, since a forget rewrites no other note's file and each forget re-checks its revision under the lock — so `a_twenty_target_forget_parses_the_partition_twice_per_target` holds unchanged (a first version that read every target twice failed it). **Why not leaving the forget's own targets out of the withdrawals**: the suggestion would stay pending, naming a forgotten note and holding a copy of its text, until someone retried. Tests: `a_forget_that_fails_forgets_the_suggestion_it_names_in_either_order` (the review's probe P4, both orders), red before and red again with the ordering disabled; the either-order test now pins the results' order (red with the sort removed).
  - **No test drove `memory forget`'s own path** (tests nit). *Pinned* (`91fe6bef`): the verb's body after connecting is `forget_verb`, emitting every line as `forget_notes` does; 21 ids over the canned server, the last read back as a pending suggestion, which is the first target of the first request — red with `forget_order` skipped in the verb.
  - **The documentation stated the shield narrower than the code** (guarantees nit 4, consistency nits 3 and 4 *(rev 3.44: this entry said "7 and 8", which are round 1's numbers for two other findings)*): the note a suggestion names is spared whatever its origin (an inferred suggestion may name a learned note) — §3.3 above, 03 §4 and §6, `ForgetMode::Forget`'s doc, and rev 3.42's clause "a row only the suggestion or learned notes cite", which §3.3 now words as built and the entry below corrects in place, marked (`beb5efcb`'s message keeps its words). The Daemon, CLI and GUI Manuals and `TECHNICAL.md` state the rule as built, for candidates as for suggestions.
  - **The Daemon Manual said the `<memory_suggestions>` line says what a suggestion changes** (consistency minor). It says the assistant suggested it, and no more: the ids, revisions, titles, who saved the target and where the suggestion came from (`PendingSuggestion::render`). The sentence now gives "what else it changes" to the Memory view, `openalpaca memory suggestions` and the in-chat card alone.
  - **Two "never the model's text" survived** (consistency nit): `ServerEvent::ToolConfirmationRequested.preview` and the SSE frame's `preview` in `chat-stream.ts` now say "composed from the notes, never from the call's arguments". Rev 3.42's entry called the two it fixed "the two remaining"; these two were left.
  - **§10's Where-built** (consistency nit): LQ11's row names `c3fb1cf8` (rev 3.42) and this round's commits; LQ17's names `c3fb1cf8` beside `13b7a3f9`, no longer "its last pins".
  - **Evidence** (session scratchpad, `memeval/l8/closeout/`): `impl.md` (*Repair round 2*), `work/rep2/` (red, green and negative-control logs, the gates).
- **rev 3.42 (2026-09-27), the L8 close-out's repair round 1 (`d515613f..c3fb1cf8` and the documentation commit that records this entry).** The close-out's first round of three reviews — tests, guarantees, consistency — as the lead listed them for repair: 8 minor findings and 3 nits, several of them one defect seen through two or three lenses. Each is fixed; none is rejected. Behavioural fixes are tested first, each new test red on the code before its fix. §3.1 row 5, §3.3, §4.4, §6.5.5, §6.5.8, §10's LQ11 row and the status line are amended in place; 03 is rev 7.13. No route, event, field, migration or index schema changes.
  - **A forget that fails on a later target stranded the suggestions it owed** (tests 1, guarantees 1, consistency 2; a regression from A3). *Fixed* (`d515613f`): rev 3.41 ran the withdrawals after the forget's loop, so a `?` inside it — a quarantined partition, a file it could not remove, a busy partition — returned before them; the notes forgotten before the failing target kept their pending suggestions, among them the chat model's copy of a forgotten note's text, which the model lists and reads and which nothing withdraws any more (a retry answers the note `not_found`). The loop is a `try_for_each`; the withdrawals run over what landed, then its error is returned. Test: `a_forget_that_fails_on_a_later_target_still_withdraws_what_it_owes`, the review's probe in both variants (a quarantined project partition, a read-only `notes/`), red before.
  - **The CLI's batched `memory forget`** (tests 2, guarantees 5, consistency 3). *Fixed* (`941ac190`): a refused request returned the error alone, so the lines of the requests before it — forgets that had landed — were never printed, nor which of the refused request's own ids the service had forgotten before the one that failed; and past 20 ids a note in one request and its suggestion in a later one were A3's order-dependent case again. Each request's lines now print as it returns; after a refusal each id of that request is read back (gone: forgotten; there: not forgotten) and the ids after it are counted as never sent; and the pending suggestions — a candidate naming a note, known from the read the prompt already makes — are sent first (a suggestion never names a suggestion, so no request withdraws one a later request names). Tests: 21 ids with the second request refused, and with the first refused after one landed; the order, pure. Red before (no lines; the given order).
  - **A2's shield covered only the named note** (guarantees 2). *Fixed, the review's option (a)* (`beb5efcb`): one turn's two edit suggestions cite that turn's row; the person confirmed one (origin `user`), the model retracted the other with `memory_forget`, and the retraction suppressed the turn's row — the note the user had just confirmed left automatic recall, unannounced (probe P5). A pending suggestion's forget now suppresses none of the sources any note of saved origin in the partition cites; a row only the suggestion or learned notes cite is still suppressed *(rev 3.43: learned notes other than the one it names — that note's rows are spared whatever its origin)*, so rule 12 and the episode writer still lose every row no saved note rests on. **Why not option (b)**, the model's retraction of its own suggestion as a withdrawal: it covers the chat model's suggestion alone, where learning's and the person's forget retire a saved note in the same way — file-level fixture 25's step 16 is exactly that shape (b3's forget suppressed 2502, which b1, confirmed at step 14, cites) — and it would suppress nothing even where no saved note cites the row. A note forgotten earlier in the same forget shields nothing and its own tombstone suppresses its rows, so the result is the same in either order (a storage test runs both). Fixture 25: b3 also cites 2503, step 16's tombstone suppresses 2503 alone, and a new step 17 reads b1 intact; the fixture fails on the old shield. Tests: a storage test and the review's probe P5 as a service test, by the tool and by the person, red before.
  - **The card's `Changes:` line could cut a validity change away** (guarantees 3). *Fixed* (`92ec9799`): one 160-byte cut in the wire's order put validity last, behind two unbounded lists, so one long alias hid an end date and the person's Approve took the note out of recall (probe P2). `changes_list` keeps every field's label and "…", gives the validity window its bytes first — both bounds at their longest fit beside every other label — and lets the key and the lists share the rest, a short one whole before a long one is cut; the line is at most 170 bytes, within the 87 each text gives up. Tests: P2's shape, and the worst-case preview extended to all five fields with a 64-character key, asserting every label and the whole window — both red on the old line.
  - **Test gaps** (tests 3, tests 4 nit). *Pinned* (`c3fb1cf8`): the P8 parity test lists the assistant's edit suggestion of a saved note whose body carries a `Replaces …` sentence (reason none from the index and from the files — `replace_reason` restored on the file path now fails it), and learning's suggestion there carries a reason of its own; the note-level `prefix` test runs both chunk orders (the prefix branch's own check removed now fails the whole-word-first order with `[Term, Prefix]`).
  - **The tq-0032 record** (consistency 1). The close-out's own B2 table shows its five header-relevance variants recover tq-0032 too. The lead's words stand; a dated note (§6.5.5) says each of those five costs the held-out set at least one row, so the ruling's premise holds for every variant that recovered the row, and 03 §0 says "every variant".
  - **Documentation** of the above: 03 §4, §6, §7 (rev 7.13); the CLI Manual (requests of up to 20, suggestions first, what a refusal reports; a pending suggestion spares every saved note's messages), the Daemon Manual (the shield, the withdrawals on a refusal, the route's row, the `Changes:` line), the GUI Manual, `TECHNICAL.md`, the forget route's doc comment. The consistency review's one-line nits are taken too: the two remaining "never the model's" (`ToolConfirmationBanner.tsx`, `security/confirmation.rs`) and §4.4's quote of DESIGN_SPEC, §4.4's "fourth line" (the line after "New:" or "Changes:"), `PREVIEW_CHANGES_TEXT_COST`'s cited test (in `92ec9799`), §6.5.8's "each puts … first" (three of the five were run on that store), and the Daemon and CLI Manuals' absolute "suppresses none" and "never re-extracted".
  - **Not given for this round, and untouched:** the tests review's nit 3 (a sub-second validity bound makes a suggestion that changes nothing at the file's precision), the guarantees review's nit 4 (a copy made before a forget is raised on the in-chat card), and the consistency review's nit 9 (four commits of the close-out leave a daemon test red; nothing is rewritten).
  - **Evidence** (session scratchpad, `memeval/l8/closeout/`): `impl.md` (*Repair round 1*), `work/rep1/` (red, green and negative-control logs, the gates).
- **rev 3.41 (2026-09-27), the L8 close-out (`f928d25e..13b7a3f9`, the documentation commit that records this entry, and `041aeaf2`).** The last open findings of the product slice's third reviews (tests, guarantees, consistency) and of the layout build's third reviews (tests, retrieval, consistency), as the lead listed them (A1–A5, B1–B4), and the lead's ruling on tq-0032. Every behavioural change is tested first — each new test failed on the code before its fix — and each fix bites under a negative control. §3.1 row 5, §3.2 S4, §3.3, §3.5, §4.4, §6.5 (intro, §6.5.2–§6.5.6, §6.5.8), §10's LQ11, LQ13 and LQ17 rows and the status line are amended in place; 03 is rev 7.12 (a contract addition: the suggestions item's `changes`), DESIGN_SPEC rev 18.
  - **A1 (major), a validity-only edit of a saved note was dropped.** *Fixed* (`f928d25e`): `edited_content` counted `valid_from`/`valid_to` as a change and sent the edit to `suggest_edit`, but S2's "replace X with X" test (`same_note`, kind, title, body, key, aliases, tags) did not, so the result was the saved note itself — nothing written, `suggested_for` none, read by the model as a done update. S2's test and S4's restatement test now compare the validity window too (`same_replacement`); §7c Q8's dedup of a store with no `supersedes` is unchanged. Pinned field by field: each of title, body, aliases, tags, key, `valid_from`, `valid_to` changed alone is a suggestion carrying it (the last two red before), and a store with the note's text and another window is a suggestion, its restatement the same one, another window a second.
  - **A2 (minor, a guarantee), forgetting the assistant's edit suggestion retired the saved note.** *Fixed at the root, in the file layer, for every pending suggestion* (`17ba8538`): a candidate's forget (`ForgetMode::Forget`) suppresses none of the sources its target — still present, not forgotten in the same call — cites. The suggestion's own sources are suppressed as before (the turn's row: a sibling learned from it follows it out, C2's accepted cost). **Why this and not the other two:** making a forget of a pending suggestion a withdrawal would also stop a forget of learning's suggestion from suppressing the user's own row, which rule 12 and the episode writer read to keep a forgotten statement from being learned or summarised again; and citing only the turn's row while pending, adding the note's rows at confirm, would change the confirm and still let a copy made after a forget carry a suppressed row back. The shield keeps round 2's intent — a confirmed copy still follows a sibling's forget out — and takes nothing of the target through a suggestion. Tests: a storage test (the target's row not suppressed, the suggestion's own row suppressed, a confirmed suggestion's forget suppressing all) and the review's probe A as a service test, by the tool and by the person. Negative control: the shield off fails exactly those two.
  - **A2's related nit, re-checked under the fix: a copy made after a forget.** The shield does not reach it — the copy is written after the forget, so the suppression does not hold for it once it is confirmed. *Fixed by refusal* (`17ba8538`): a `ModelTool` `memory_update edit` of a note of origin `user`, `external` or `imported` that is `source_forgotten` is `authority_conflict`, naming the Memory view and `openalpaca memory edit`, before the suggestion path — and before the direct path, where a `review_after`-only edit of an `external` note would have lifted the suppression at once. An existing refusal code, so no §7a refusal is added. `memory_update`'s description says so.
  - **A3 (minor), a forget's result depended on its targets' order.** *Fixed* (`17ba8538`): `forget` withdrew a forgotten note's suggestions inside its loop, so `[W, S]` withdrew S before its turn — `not_found`, its row never suppressed — while `[S, W]` forgot both. The withdrawals now run after the loop, per partition, from the view that partition's last forget returned (so the 20-target parse count, 2 per target, holds; each withdrawal re-checks its revision under the lock). The CLI's `memory forget` sends every id in one `POST /v1/memories/forget` (20 per request) where it sent one `DELETE` per id, which would have kept the order dependence across requests, and prints each outcome. Tests: both orders forget both notes and the sibling of S's row is `source_forgotten` (red on `[W, S]` before); the CLI sends one request with the ids in order (red: a second `DELETE` before).
  - **A4 (minor), the card showed a borrowed reason and hid a metadata change.** *Fixed* (`46d5008c`). The assistant's own suggestion (origin `extracted_user` with no `observed_at`, LQ14's datum) gives no reason read from its body, on the card, the CLI line and the preview. **The model's `reason` is not carried**: it is a tool argument, shown on `memory_update`'s own card, and no datum of the note — a new frontmatter field would be a §7a change, and writing it into the body would put the model's sentence into the text the person confirms — so "or none" it is. The suggestions item gains `changes` — each of `fact_key`, `aliases`, `tags`, `valid_from`, `valid_to` whose values differ from the target's, `{field, from, to}` with lists — drawn as one row per field in the Memory view, a `changes:` line in `openalpaca memory suggestions`, and a `Changes:` line in the preview after "New:" (160 bytes; the texts give up 87 bytes each while it is there, so the 1 KiB bound and the tail hold at the worst case — a negative control, the texts keeping their budget, loses the tail); `memory show` prints the validity bounds, which it did not. Kind is not shown: the extractor classifies each item afresh, an edit cannot change it, and it says nothing about the user a person decides on. Every changed value is escaped with the one-line escape and counted by the one count (`suggestion_hidden` gains the values; `hidden-chars.json` a third `suggestion_cases` entry, held on both sides). The service test helper that models learning's suggestion now stamps `observed_at`, as learning does, and so does the daemon's route-test helper (`041aeaf2`, found by the first workspace run after this entry's commit). Red on the change's parent: the service test (reason, preview), two CLI tests, two GUI tests.
  - **A5 (nits).** `edit_sources` cites the note's rows first and the turn's row where the cap of eight leaves room (`17ba8538`; a test at the cap, where the turn's row used to push out the note's last row, so a sibling's forget left the confirmed copy in recall — red before). The tool result says the user answers the suggestion "Replace, or Keep mine to decline" (`9ab4898d`; the tool test pins the words, red on the old text). "Never the model's" becomes "composed from the notes, never from the call's arguments" in 03 §7, DESIGN_SPEC §3.14 (with the assistant's variant and the `Changes:` line), the GUI Manual, `memory_confirm`'s description and its preview hook's doc — and the Daemon Manual and API_MAP, which said it too. The doc comments: `routes/memories.rs`' `refusal` (four refusals name a next step, not one), its module doc (`changes`) and the forget route's (the withdrawals' order); `error.rs`' `NotASuggestion` and `AuthorityConflict`; `MemoryConfirmTool`'s. §10's *Where built* gains repair round 2's LQ11 and LQ13 commits and this close-out's. The Daemon Manual's "Two more the envelope can carry" (it lists three) reads "Three more".
  - **B1 (tests).** *Pinned* (`13b7a3f9`): the three-character threshold (`tea` finds `teams`, `x_abc` finds `x_abcd`; PREFIX_MIN_CHARS 4 fails two tests), the note-level `prefix` rule (a word whole in one chunk drops `prefix` from another, index and scan; the removal off fails it), the cap's boundary (exactly 256 whole-word bodies are added; `<` fails it) and `Literal::word_probes`' split (a glued literal found spaced hides its parts, index and scan; unsplit fails it). The literal test's name and doc, fixture 22's `design` and notes, and the parts-fallback comments in `search.rs`, `query.rs` and the tests say a number ends a prefix and "found", not "matches".
  - **B2 (retrieval, minor), the header relevance's length bias.** *Kept, and its cost stated* (§6.5.3, §6.5.5, §6.5.8). Five length-free variants were built and run through the retrieval proxy against `13b7a3f9` (which equals `layout/after/` row for row): no header relevance; relevance only for a chunk holding a word or literal inside a token alone; the third review's v2; and the share of the question's words the header holds, for every candidate or for the header leg's. Each is a net loss (−2 to −4 rows with every relied-on note delivered, −1 to −2 listed or delivered), from ties at the delivery cut the header's length now decides (§6.5.5), so none is taken; a unit test written for the substring-only variant (the controlled store's "The user's birthday" first among 101) was red on the build and green on the variant, and is dropped with it. The §6.5 text on the cliff at 256 is corrected: it fires on body length alone (§6.5.2, §6.5.4, the cliff test's doc).
  - **B3 (docs).** O5 and `prefix=` are no longer "not needed for the target": §6.4's decision rule is met, the service's recall target (264.5 ms p95 at 100K) is not, LQ15 set the design point at ~30K, and both stay open as levers (§6.5.5, §6.5.8); the 3.4 ms sub-bullet sits beside the p95s it qualifies. Every "a last token is a prefix only from three characters" is corrected (§6.5.6, fixture 22, the tests). 03's rev 7.11 entry names its §0 amendment. §6.5's intro dates its "today" citations to `f6f40feb` and gives the moved ones at `13b7a3f9`. The Devanagari consonant-skeleton hits the file scan does not return are recorded as residue (§6.5.3).

  - **The retrieval proxy on `13b7a3f9`** (both modes, both sets, system D, no model, networking denied): 257 / 293, 288, 82 / 94, 94 — `layout/after/` and repair round 2 row for row on relied-on notes delivered and listed and per-turn hits (1,240 rows). The product changes touch no recall path.
  - **Evidence** (session scratchpad, `memeval/l8/closeout/`): `impl.md`, `work/` (red, green and negative-control logs; the variants' comparison), `proxy-pre/`, `proxy-b2/`, `proxy-v1/`…`proxy-v4/`, `proxy.sh`, `proxy_compare.py`, `variants.py`.
- **rev 3.40 (2026-09-27), LQ17's layout build, repair round 2 (`7076f653` and the documentation commit that records this entry).** A second round of three reviews of the build — tests, retrieval, consistency — gave the lead's list of findings for repair: 1 major, 5 minor and 3 nits. Each is fixed, or recorded where the finding allowed it; none is rejected. The reviews' other one-line nits are taken too (below). Behavioural fixes are tested first: four storage tests failed on the repaired build's code — `a_literal_found_only_in_a_longer_form_still_routes_its_parts`, `a_literal_ending_in_a_number_finds_the_text_it_begins` and two query unit tests — and each fix bites under a negative control. §6.5, §6.5.1–§6.5.7, the status line and §10's LQ17 row are amended in place; 03 is rev 7.11. Both code changes are on the query side, so the index schema stays 5.
  - **M1 (major, retrieval), a longer form hid an identifier's parts.** *Fixed, by the review's second option.* `literal_has_hit` decided whether a literal was found with the prefix phrase the search sends — the prototype's rule too — so `dark modes`, `code reviews`, `time zones` and `side projects` in one note kept `dark`, `review`, `time` and `project` from being searched, and a note holding one of them whole was lost in a store of any size; §6.5.4 did not name it. It now decides as schema 3 did — the exact phrase, or a substring either trigram table holds — plus a longer form the prefix phrase finds that holds the literal verbatim in its body, which keeps fixture 26's step 9 (`memory_file` in `memory_files`) where the review's first option would have searched `memory` and `file`. The prefix phrase still finds candidates and gives `phrase`. `Literal::word_probes` builds the two word-table probes, and the file scan decides the same way (`ChunkText::finds_literal`). On the review's probe every case is back at schema 3's rank; over the 620 questions at the index level one hit list changes (tq-0117: schema 3's second hit returns, the relied-on note stays 1st), and the relied-on ranks within 2, 8, 10 and 20 stay 447, 509, 514 and 541 (§6.5.2).
  - **m1 (minor, retrieval), a beginning ending in a short number.** *Fixed, by the review's first option:* a literal whose last token is a number is a prefix whatever its length — `10:30` finds `10:30am`, `v1.2` finds `v1.2beta`, `3.12` finds `3.12rc1`, and `2026-09-15` is a `phrase` again. The prefix merge is measured at 100K notes of the learned mix, where nearly every note carries a date: +5 ms for `v1.2`, +13 ms for `2026.2`, +38 ms for `09.2`, which now finds the thousands of dates it begins, and −9 ms for `2026-09-1`; nothing at 30K of the average mix (§6.5.2). §6.5.4's sentence on beginnings says which are found and which are not (an identifier ending in one or two letters; a number inside a word).
  - **F1 (minor, tests), the cliff at 256 unpinned, its reason wrong.** *Pinned and recorded:* the review's test, as `a_body_word_held_by_more_than_the_cap_adds_none_at_the_cap` — it passes, and fails with the guard off or the body query's limit at 256. §6.5.2's sentence that the first query "keeps the best of them" is corrected, and §6.5.4 names the class with the review's probe: a word held whole in more than 256 eligible bodies reaches the candidates only through the prefix query's own 256, which can hold none of those notes when the longer words sit in titles. The ranked body query of §6.5.8 would close it, at +34 ms of p95 at 100K; it is put to the lead as a measured trade-off.
  - **m2 (minor, retrieval), which query shapes meet the latency rule.** *Recorded:* §6.5.5 states the rule for the bench's questions and adds the review's measurements of whole messages, the shape the recall sends — at 100K about 270–320 ms of search alone, half the current layout's.
  - **M1 (minor, consistency), "space-separated script".** *Fixed:* what is lost is drawn by what `unicode61` splits into words — Latin, Cyrillic, Greek, Arabic, Hebrew … — in 03 §5 step 4, §6.5.4, §6.5.1's line, both module docs and fixture 26.
  - **M2 (minor, consistency), "§6.4's target is met with room".** *Fixed:* the bullet names §6.4's decision rule (index search + verification), and §6.5.5 carries the service bench's recall line (10K 33.7 → 29.8 ms, inside 04 §5's gate; 100K 397.6 → 264.5 ms, above §6.4's proposed 100 ms), the rebuild times, and the open peak-memory finding that was only in rev 3.38's entry.
  - **F2 (nit, tests), the glued word pinned one way.** *Fixed:* the glued-word test gains a note holding `版本` and `python` apart, which neither the index nor the file scan returns; checking the tokens in any order fails it.
  - **N1 and N2 (nits, consistency).** *Fixed:* §6.5's intro names 03 revs 7.10 and 7.11; 03 §0 names both repairs; §6.5.7's tool-description bullet quotes the widened wording; fixture 23's `design` dates the Devanagari rule to 03 rev 7.10 and its notes count five; the citations of `open` and `open_existing` (`memory_index/mod.rs:557`, `:599`), `commands/memory.rs:1829`, `W_TRIGRAM` (`search.rs:221`) and, in CLAUDE.md, `INDEX_FILE_NAME` (`:58`).
  - **Nits the reviews raised that this round also took**, each a line: the Tai Le range is pinned by a letter in the unit list (tests, F3; removing it now fails that test); the body queries change 15 top-8 sets at 30K of the dense mix, not none (F4); the index held-out total is 257 or 258 over seven runs, hq-0045 and hq-0046 being ties at the excerpt cut (F5); the body queries' cost at 30K, +1.5–2 ms of median, and "how do I get to work?" at 100K, 36th and 28th (retrieval, n1). Not taken: the redesign README's stale status line and table (consistency, N3), which is more than a line; it stays for the lead.
  - **The retrieval proxy on `7076f653`** (both modes, both sets, system D, no model, networking denied, the binary built from the committed code): every relied-on note delivered 257 and 288 on the held-out set, 82 and 94 on the tuning set; listed or delivered 293 and 94; relied-on notes delivered 308, 345, 97 and 113 — repair round 1's totals, and row for row its relied-on notes delivered and listed and its per-turn hits on all 1,240 rows. `memory_used` changes on tq-0117 in both modes (the M1 fix: schema 3's second note back beside the relied-on one), and elsewhere only among tied notes, which trade order and places at the cut as between repeated runs of either layout (hq-0045, -0046, -0169, -0170, -0254, -0418, -0430, tq-0004); relied-on counts never. So index tuning's listed-or-delivered (95 → 94, tq-0032) still awaits the lead's ruling.
  - **Evidence** (session scratchpad, `memeval/l8/layout/r2/`): `logs/` (the red run, the negative controls, the gates), `probe/` (the review's harness on schema 3, the repaired build and this one: `new`, `cases`, `ver`, `indic`, `gold`, and the timings), `proxy/` and `proxy-compare.txt`, and the edit scripts.
- **rev 3.39 (2026-09-27), LQ17's layout build, repair round 1 (`2684af55` and the documentation commit that records this entry).** Three reviews of the build — tests, retrieval, consistency — gave nine findings for repair: 2 major, 6 minor and 1 nit. Each is fixed or, where the finding allowed it, recorded; none is rejected. Behavioural fixes are tested first: eight new or changed storage tests failed on the build's code (with only the new query builder's signature stubbed), and each fix is shown to bite under a negative control. §6.5 is amended in place — §6.5.1's unsegmented scripts, §6.5.2's queries at the cap, §6.5.3, §6.5.4's letters and digits, §6.5.5's tables, §6.5.6–§6.5.8 — and the status line and §10's LQ17 row; 03 is rev 7.10, 04 rev 4.7 and 08 draft 5.
  - **M1 (major), a whole word in a body crowded out at the cap.** *Fixed, by the review's option (b) confined to the cap.* When the word table's query fills its cap, each term it sent as a prefix is now also searched alone as a whole word in bodies (`{body} : "w"`), unranked, reading at most 257 rows; its chunks join the candidates when there are at most 256 of them. The review's probe on the bench's corpus at 30,000 notes gets back all 8 lost notes — 7 at schema 3's rank (1st or 2nd), and "how do I get to work?" at 30th, since a chunk found only at the cap takes no word relevance (§6.5.3); at 10,000, `mark` and `doc` are 1st again. The review's option (a), one whole-word query over every column, lost two header notes on the scale proxy, where its own cap filled with body matches. A ranked body query beside the header one recovered what the per-term queries recover, for +11 ms of p95 at 30K and +34 ms at 100K; the per-term queries cost about 1 ms at 10K and nothing beyond the runs' noise above it (§6.5.5). The scale proxy moves four relied-on notes back to the rank schema 3 gave them — each had ranked above a bench note that the prefix had crowded out — so four of its cells lose one row against rev 3.37's table (§6.5.5). §6.5.2's reason ("the notes a prefix crowds out are … a title, an alias, a tag") is corrected, as the review asked.
  - **M2 (major), scripts `unicode61` breaks at their vowel signs.** *Fixed.* `query::is_unsegmented` adds Devanagari and the other Brahmic scripts, Thaana, the Philippine scripts, Limbu, Sundanese, Batak, Lepcha, Cham, Meetei Mayek and Miao (words built with vowel signs), and Tibetan, Yi, Javanese, Balinese, Buginese, Tai Le, New Tai Lue, Tai Tham and Tai Viet (written without spaces); `SCHEMA_VERSION` is 5, so a schema-4 cache is rebuilt. Rather than deriving the predicate at run time, a test derives it at build time: it indexes every character `query::tokens` keeps inside a word in the bundled SQLite and fails on any `unicode61` breaks a word at that the ranges miss, but for the listed vowel points of Hebrew, Arabic, Syriac and Samaritan (unpointed in ordinary text) and a few combining letters and symbols. Fixture 23 gains note 005 and step 14 (`दोस्त` finds `दोस्तों`, `phrase`); a storage test covers Hindi, Tamil, Bengali, Yi and Javanese in the index and the file scan. On the review's probes every such query is back to schema 3's hit and reason — `मेरे दोस्त कौन हैं` 10th, as there. §6.5.1's and §6.5.4's claims are corrected.
  - **F1 (minor), a query word glued across scripts.** *Fixed as proposed:* `ChunkText::has_token` also accepts a word whose split tokens stand in sequence in a field, so `python版本` is a `term` in `Python 版本` and in `我们的python版本`, in the index and the file scan alike (a new test, the review's probe among its cases). Chinese dates as queries (`2026年`, `7月15日`) report `term` now, where both layouts said `trigram`.
  - **F2 (minor), no test pinned "a chunk the cap query alone kept takes no word relevance".** *Fixed:* the header and the body tests at the cap assert the kept note's score is its coverage and authority exactly; giving those chunks the query's rank fails both.
  - **F3 (nit), trigram relevance as a maximum.** *Fixed:* a CJK term inside runs of a note's title and body scores one trigram relevance weight; a sum fails it.
  - **m1 (minor), rev 3.38's "§6.5.5's figures stand as the prototype's".** *Measured on the build:* with the prototype's harness applied to the build's own code, the build before this repair returns the prototype's (E6) rankings exactly on the scale proxy's 1,860 query-scenarios, the learning lookup's 300 rows, L8's coverage search's 166 and the 200 bench queries' top-8 sets; §6.5.5 now gives the repaired build's figures, and the 30K and dense benches are the build's (two runs each). Rev 3.38's equality to E6 on the retrieval proxy said nothing about the cap path, as the review said.
  - **m2 (minor), letters and digits run together.** *Recorded* in §6.5.4's *Whom it costs*, with the review's cases, so the alias remedy is applied to them knowingly.
  - **Fixture 26 at slice 3 (minor).** *Fixed by a core test*, `the_body_only_prefix_fixture_recalls_through_the_service`, which checks step 2's `memory_used` (reason `recall`) and the header's `match=prefix` over the fixture's seven notes; the README row says so.
  - **CLAUDE.md's rebuild warning (minor).** *Fixed:* the first start rebuilds the index to schema 5.
  - **Nits the reviews raised and this round also took**, each a line: rev 3.38's "`memory_used` varies between runs only in the order of tied notes" is loose — tied notes at the cut also trade places (hq-0045, -0046, -0254, -0430), relied counts never; the core test comment that `prefer` reaches `prefers` "only through the trigram table"; CLAUDE.md's "the trigram companion" and its revision pointers; 03 Q12's "the build measures it again", now answered; 04's and 08's status lines; the fixture README's slice-2 paragraph on `excerpt_contains`; and one wording of the scripts in every manual and the tool description.
  - **The retrieval proxy on the repaired build** (both modes, both sets, system D, no model, networking denied): every relied-on note delivered 257 and 288 on the held-out set, 82 and 94 on the tuning set; listed or delivered 293 and 94; relied-on notes delivered 308, 345, 97 and 113 — the build's (rev 3.38) totals, and row for row its relied-on notes delivered and listed and its per-turn hits on all 1,240 rows. Nothing in the repair reaches 83 notes: the cap is never reached, no gold note carries a newly unsegmented script, and no question has a word glued across scripts; at the index level the 620 questions' hit lists are identical too. So index tuning's listed-or-delivered (95 → 94, tq-0032) still awaits the lead's ruling (§6.5.5).
  - **Evidence** (session scratchpad, `memeval/l8/layout/r1/`): `logs/` (the red run, the negative controls, the gates), `out/` and `out-ranked/` (the scale proxy, learning, coverage and probe outputs, per-term and ranked), `bench-pt/` and `bench-ranked/` (the benches), `proxy/` (the dry-run reports), the scripts, and the worktree `r1-wt/` with the harness.
- **rev 3.38 (2026-09-27), LQ17's layout built (phase 2 of the LQ17 slice, `457c1122`).** §6.5 is built as rev 3.37 states it: memory index schema 4, `MatchReason::Prefix`, the fixtures of §6.5.6 and every surface of §6.5.7. No design text changes; the status line, §10's LQ17 row and 03 (rev 7.9) record the build.
  - **Tests first.** The new and changed tests were run against the schema-3 layout, with only a `Prefix` variant added so they compiled: 14 storage tests (fixtures 21, 22 and 26 among them) and 8 core tests failed; fixture 23 and the new test of a CJK term inside a run passed, as the regression guards §6.5.6 says they are. On the build: the storage library 705 passed, the core library 2,106, the workspace 4,341 with none failing. Three rules nothing else pins were each broken on purpose: with the whole-word query at the cap disabled, its test fails (the tag `work` note leaves the candidates); with every literal's last token a prefix, 26/8 and 22/19 fail; with no literal a prefix, 26/9 fails.
  - **Deviations, each small:**
    - `query::is_unsegmented` also covers the Khmer symbols and the two Myanmar extended blocks — the same scripts; the prototype had the four base blocks. The evaluation data has no such character.
    - FTS5's contentless-delete tables fold a replacement's delete into the old segment and merge a segment most of whose rows are gone, so the maintenance test's replacement rounds left `fts_word` and `fts_tri_head` at one segment. The test adds three batches of new notes, so all three tables have segments to merge; the core idle-tick test's notes gain a Chinese line for `fts_tri`.
    - Tests §6.5.7 did not list: the scale test that copies a stray `note` row names the two new columns; the empty-body test also searches the header (`term`, and a `trigram` substring of the title); the file-scan test adds a glued-word note, a header-substring note and the queries `commit`, `now`, `presso`, `check`, `发版前`, `memory_file`; the FTS integrity check runs with rank 0, which is all a contentless table can check.
    - `literal_has_hit` probes each table with the MATCH string the search itself sends, and the ranker's substring check holds the three-character rule; the prototype built both by hand. The prototype's scratch hooks (`LQ17_*`, the hit dump) are not carried over.
  - **The retrieval proxy on the build** (both recall modes, both question sets; system D, no model, outbound networking denied): every relied-on note delivered 254 → 257 and 286 → 288 on the held-out set, 82 → 82 and 93 → 94 on the tuning set; listed or delivered 293 → 293 and 95 → 94; relied-on notes delivered 305 → 308, 344 → 345, 97 → 97, 112 → 113. Every row's relied-on notes delivered and listed, and its per-turn hits, equal the prototype's (E6) run; the rows lost are §6.5.5's five (hq-0131, hq-0152, hq-0228, hq-0318, tq-0032), for the reasons given there. Three index-mode runs of each set agree row for row; `memory_used` varies between runs only in the order of tied notes (hq-0045 and its kin), as it does on the current layout.
  - **The benches**, one run each, release, `/usr/bin/time -l`, against a pristine copy of the schema-3 code (`af7a1414`) built and run the same day on the same machine — the L8 bench logs predate 80 commits, the scale slice among them, so they are no baseline for the service bench:
    - index, 10K average: 109.2 → 40.2 MiB; rebuild 4.6 → 1.0 s; search + verify p50 / p95 / max 5.4 / 10.1 / 15.5 → 5.0 / 8.7 / 11.0 ms; peak RSS 247 → 142 MB;
    - index, 100K average: 1,065.5 → 366.6 MiB; rebuild 53.9 → 15.2 s; 74.0 / 154.0 / 222.1 → 25.6 / 48.6 / 70.3 ms; 691 → 685 MB;
    - index, 100K learned: 531.7 → 219.7 MiB; rebuild 30.0 → 9.4 s; 17.9 / 56.4 / 85.4 → 8.8 / 27.4 / 40.0 ms; 571 → 531 MB;
    - service, 10K: recall p50 / p95 28.6 / 33.7 → 27.0 / 29.8 ms (parse cache on); open with a missing cache (the rebuild) 3.1 → 1.1 s; cache 88.3 → 39.7 MiB; `learning_view` p95 41.6 → 37.8 ms;
    - service, 100K: recall 342.3 / 397.6 → 242.4 / 264.5 ms; the rebuild 40.9 → 16.7 s; cache 835.3 → 362.2 MiB; `learning_view` p95 487.7 → 353.7 ms; a second run of each gave recall and the rebuild within 10 % of the first.
    - **One open finding: the 100K service bench's peak memory rose.** Two runs of each: peak RSS 1,955 and 1,666 MB on schema 3, 2,157 and 2,145 MB on schema 4; peak footprint 998 and 771 against 1,193 and 1,197 MB. The index benches show no such rise (100K: 691 → 685 MB, footprint 414 → 409), nor does the 10K service bench (293 → 232 MB). Which phase of the service bench holds the extra memory is not measured; it is recorded for a follow-up, not explained.
  - **Not measured again by this phase:** the scale proxy at 30,000 notes, the learning lookup, L8's coverage search, the 30K and dense benches, and the 200 bench queries' hit sets. The prototype's harness for them is scratch-only; the build equals the prototype on every proxy row, and on the 10K and 100K index benches within run-to-run noise (40.2 / 366.6 / 219.7 MiB exactly), so §6.5.5's figures for them stand as the prototype's.
  - **Evidence** (session scratchpad, `memeval/l8/layout/`): `impl/` (the red runs, the gates, the scripts), `after/` (the proxy reports, three index runs of each set), `bench-after/` and `bench-base/` (the bench logs), `base-src/` (the schema-3 copy), and the report `impl.md`.
- **rev 3.37 (2026-09-27), LQ17's index layout revision, answering its review (design text only; phase 1 of the LQ17 slice).** A sceptic reviewed rev 3.36 and asked for fixes: 2 major findings, 6 minor, 3 nits. Each is answered below. Most change the design, measured again on the prototype — its final form is the variant the notes call E6. None is rejected outright; where the fix taken differs from the one the review proposed, the entry says why. §6.5 is rewritten in place; §10's LQ17 row and the status line are amended; rev 3.36's entry carries an inline correction; 03 is rev 7.8, and 04 is rev 4.6.
  - **1 (major), a Latin substring still ranked inside mixed chunks.** Rev 3.36 said a body trigram hit counts in ranking only for a term carrying an unsegmented character, but its ranker credited a Latin substring (`W_TRIGRAM` and the `trigram` reason) in any chunk carrying one CJK character; only the body table's BM25 was withheld. The template headings then matched Chinese and mixed notes and no English one — 287 question–note pairs over 25 questions — and hq-0090 and hq-0120 showed it, as the review's reruns found. *Fixed, by another route than the review proposed.* The review proposed matching a Latin term in an unsegmented body only on tokens cut at script boundaries, in the ranker and the file scan, and keeping a retrieval leg on the body table for glued Latin such as `和commit`. The revision cuts at script boundaries in the word table itself (`split_scripts`, on the index side and the query side), so a glued word is a word: `term`, with the word table's BM25. The body table is then searched only for terms carrying an unsegmented character, and the retrieval leg is gone. That leg would have filled its own cap with every Chinese chunk under a `## Knowledge` heading, for the ranker to throw away. Both of the review's pins are fixture steps (26/6, `now`; 26/7, `commit`), red on the current layout and on rev 3.36's prototype. The proxy is unchanged by this, row for row, but for the known tie hq-0045.
  - **2 (major), the accuracy gate never reached the candidate cap.** *Measured, and it found a loss, now fixed.* The proxy's 620 questions were run at the index level over the gold notes among 30,000 bench notes, and with the 319 notes two L8 runs learned (§6.5.5).
    - With rev 3.36's single word leg, two relied-on notes left the candidate pool. Each was found only by the tag `work`, crowded out by `work*` → `worker`, `workspace`.
    - A larger cap did not bring them back (512: neither; 1,024: one of them).
    - The whole-word query at the cap does: the prefix terms, as whole words, in titles, aliases and tags. With it no relied-on note leaves the pool; 124 against the current layout's 126, and 129 against 132 with the learned notes.
    - A whole-word query over every column did the same, but cost 32 ms at the p95 at 100K (76 ms against 44), so the query is confined to the header.
    - The 200 bench queries' lost hits were checked against an uncapped run of each layout (§6.5.5). 136 of the 149 score at or below the revision's own 8th hit, 129 of them within one substring hit's weight. The other 13 are the cap's doing, which drops 7 of the revision's new hits on the current layout the same way.
  - **3 (minor), two retrieval consumers unmeasured.** *Measured; nothing is lost.*
    - The learning lookup, with no model: each corpus session searched as `learning_view` searches it, over the gold notes of the sessions before it. The 10 changed notes are offered on both layouts. The restatements go from 13 to 14 of 15, and from 13 to 14 within the turn's own five hits.
    - L8's coverage search, on the two L8 stores whose project partition exists: every judged match the current code returns among its five hits, the revision returns too (70 of 70, 68 of 68).
    - L8's figures are pre-LQ17, as §6.5.7 has the reports say.
  - **4 (minor), contentless tables and no trigger.** *Adopted:* one `AFTER DELETE ON chunk` trigger removes a chunk's FTS rows, and the incremental-equals-rebuilt tests compare `fts5vocab` contents keyed by note id and ordinal (§6.5.1, §6.5.7). Both are verified on SQLite 3.51: the trigger and a cascade, `fts5vocab` on a contentless table. One part of the finding is corrected: no path deletes a `note` row relying on the cascade today — every delete removes the chunk rows first (`mod.rs:1544-1585`) — so the hazard was latent, not a defect.
  - **5 (minor), fixtures that no longer kept their invariants.** *Fixed* (§6.5.6):
    - 22 gains step 19 (`x_ab*`), which tells an injected `*` from the index's own prefix, and its notes text is rewritten.
    - 21's texts are corrected: its `editor` was always a whole token of the title.
    - 12's `design` field quotes the revised step 4.
    - The two unguarded routing rules are pinned by 26/8 (`partner's` stays exact) and 26/9 (`memory_file` finds `memory_files`), each shown to fail with its rule broken, and by 26/7 (Latin glued to Chinese).
  - **6 (minor), four inaccurate statements.** *All corrected.*
    - (a) A prefix hit's coverage equals a substring hit's, but its relevance is the word table's BM25 at twice the weight — tq-0032's mechanism (§6.5.3).
    - (b) Index tuning's listed-or-delivered falls 95 → 94, deterministically. Rev 3.36 counted that as no net loss; it is a net loss of one row on one of eight totals, which the rule treats as a stop. §6.5.5 states it for the lead, with the one measured way to recover it (E4) and what that costs on the held-out set.
    - (c) A term carrying an unsegmented character is now never a prefix, so `用户喜欢` reports `trigram` like `简洁的回答` in the same run (23/6).
    - (d) The citation is `search.rs:837` / `:928`.
  - **7 (minor), a bench that understated headers and morphology.** *Measured:* a dense mix — 4.8 aliases and 115 B of header per note, inflected English with 3-letter stems, half the query words bare stems — and a 30K point (§6.5.5). The review estimated 40–50 MiB more at 100K. The dense mix costs 137 MiB more than the average mix: 504 MiB, 4.0× the body bytes, against the current layout's 1,225 MiB on the same corpus. The conclusion holds.
  - **8 (minor), stale or unstated records.** *All fixed:*
    - 04's risk row;
    - `is_unsegmented` and the split named as part of the index's contents and of the `SCHEMA_VERSION` doc comment;
    - the upgrade and downgrade path (§6.5.7);
    - the loss described as ordinary text in German and Swedish compounds and in Arabic and Hebrew attached forms, not only as English compounds and camelCase (§6.5.4).
  - **9 (nits).** *All three taken:*
    - `memory_tools/tests.rs:491` asserts the exact reasons;
    - a stop word is never a prefix (22/20: `and` does not find `Android`);
    - the damaged-cache test also damages a `chunk` page.
  - **Evidence** (session scratchpad, `memeval/l8/layout/`):
    - `proto/`: the patches from E2 to E6 (`patch_e3.py` … `patch_e6.py`), the saved variants, `stepcheck.py`, and the bench logs (`bench/r2-*`, `r3-*`);
    - `scale/`: the harness (`proto/harness/`), its job files, outputs and analyzers — scale proxy, learning lookup, coverage;
    - `work/`: `infix_loss_e3.py`, `capcheck.py`, `rowdiff.py`;
    - the review's own scripts and reruns in the scratchpad's `sceptic/`.
- **rev 3.36 (2026-09-27), LQ17's index layout revision: §6.5 (design text only; phase 1 of the LQ17 slice).** The owner's LQ17 ("write the layout revision") is written as §6.5 and measured on a prototype built in a scratch copy of `f6f40feb`, outside the repository; phase 2 builds it, after a review of this text. Nothing is built by this revision.
  - **The layout** (§6.5.1): index schema 4; one normalized copy of each chunk's text in `chunk`; the header once per note on `note`; three contentless FTS tables — `fts_word` over everything, a new `fts_tri_head` over titles, aliases and tags, and `fts_tri` over the bodies of chunks in scripts `unicode61` cannot split (CJK, Thai, Lao, Myanmar, Khmer); `SearchHit.excerpt` removed.
  - **Routing and ranking** (§6.5.2–§6.5.3; *revised by rev 3.37*): a `unicode61` prefix query for every term whose last token has three or more characters; a body trigram hit ranked only for a term carrying an unsegmented character; `MatchReason` gains `prefix`; `W_PREFIX` = `W_TRIGRAM`; the file scan agrees with the index.
  - **The measured case** (§6.5.4–§6.5.5):
    - the retrieval-proxy baseline on the current layout in both recall modes, over the held-out and tuning questions, with its repeatability;
    - the prototype against it: no net loss, and every lost row explained *(corrected in rev 3.37: index tuning's listed-or-delivered falls from 95 to 94, tq-0032 — a net loss of one row on that total, now stated for the lead to rule on)*;
    - the five variants measured and not taken;
    - the 641 infix matches the layout drops, 452 of them the note template's own headings;
    - the three index benches before and after, and the 200 bench queries' hit sets at 10K.
  - **The fixtures** (§6.5.6): 21's step 1 reason becomes `prefix`; a new 26 is the body-only variant and pins the infix boundary; 23 gains a mixed-script step; the index runner reads excerpts from the note. All are run on the prototype, red on the current layout where the behaviour changes.
  - **Amended in place:** §6.4's LR16 paragraph (a pointer to §6.5, and a correction of its fixture estimate: 23's excerpt check is a `read`, which the index runner skips), §10's LQ17 row and the status line.
  - **03 is rev 7.7:** §0, §5 step 4, §7a `MatchReason`, and §7c Q9 and Q12.
  - **Evidence** (session scratchpad, `memeval/l8/layout/`): `design.md` (the notes), `baseline/` (the four proxy reports and their repeats), `proto/` (the prototype, its variants, the bench logs, the rank debug runs, and the diffs against `f6f40feb`), `work/` (the scripts).
- **rev 3.35 (2026-09-27), the L8 product slice's repair round 2 (`04df5bce..1a6a69b8` and the documentation commit that records this entry).** Three reviews of rev 3.34 — tests, guarantees, consistency. Of what they raised, eight findings were given for repair — 5 minor and 3 nits (one nit raised by two reviews) — and each is fixed or, where the finding allowed it, recorded; none is rejected. The reviews' other nits were not given for this round (a file id with its high bit set failing the bulk listing, a prose quote still taken where the answer has no JSON of its own, a bare array with one non-item entry, the flaky `scale_tests` run, `memory_update`'s description overstating an edit's protection, the origin label's stated reason). Behavioural fixes test first and each is shown to bite under a negative control. §2.4 rule 1, §3.1 row 5, §3.3, §4.2 rule 5, §4.4 and §10's LQ16 row are amended in place; revs 3.33, 3.34 and rev 3.5's note on the suggestions item ("stays a candidate") carry inline corrections; 03 is rev 7.6.
  - **LQ13, a tag inside the answer's JSON is not a marker** (`04df5bce`; tests, minor). Rev 3.34 looked for `<think>` and `</think>` anywhere before any fenced read, so a fenced answer whose own JSON quoted a tag — the form read before LQ13 — was unreadable: items or a summary about stripping those tags failed three times at temperature 0 and the window was skipped, and a run's extraction yielded nothing. `think_markers` finds the tags outside every complete JSON value, and the whole answer read as exactly one fenced block comes first. The round's refusals stand, and an unclosed `<think>` whose draft quotes a `</think>` is still no answer. Tests: items, summary and run, red on rev 3.34's code.
  - **The prose shape filter is pinned** (`04df5bce`; tests, minor): a quoted `{"items": [...]}` with no item, alone in prose, is refused — a unit case, and a flush that holds the window (`FlushStop::BadOutput`, cursor unchanged). Task extraction's read is lifted into `run_answer`, whose test pins the twin. With both filters removed, the three tests fail.
  - **The chat model's edit suggestion and a forget** (`dffe7413`; guarantees, minor). An edit's suggestion is a copy of the saved note's text but cited only the turn's row, so (A) a forget of a sibling citing the note's row left the confirmed copy of that row's text in recall, and (B) a forget of the note left the copy a candidate the model could list and read. It now cites the turn's row and then the note's sources, at most eight; and a forget withdraws the pending suggestions naming the forgotten note (§3.3), learning's included — none can be confirmed any more. Because such a suggestion may now cite only the copied note's older rows, the card and the line date the assistant's suggestion by the day it was saved (§4.4), as the Memory view and the CLI do. Tests: A, B and the date, each red on rev 3.34's code and each failing with its fix reverted alone.
  - **Rule 5 as it reads since LQ11** (`1a6a69b8`; consistency, minor): `memory_update`'s confirm refusal called the chat model's own suggestion "a suggestion from the user's own words"; it now says "a suggestion waiting for the user". `not_a_suggestion` states rule 5 ("a suggestion that cites a message the user typed"), and the doc comments of `in_chat.rs`, `error.rs` and `memory_tools.rs` follow; 03 §4 and §7's `memory_confirm` row too.
  - **`refusal`'s doc** (`8256c364`; tests nit, consistency nit): `INDEX_REBUILDING_MESSAGE` stood between `refusal`'s doc and the function. It now stands above with its own doc, which says the timeline is refused, not that it waits; `refusal`'s sentence about "the client's words" is narrowed to a revision conflict.
  - **A store naming saved W under saved W2's key** (guarantees, nit): recorded, not built, in 03 §4 and §3.1 row 5 — shared with learning's `replaces`, so a fix of the tool path alone would leave the two unlike.
  - **Records** (consistency, minor): rev 3.34's count is corrected in place, and its unrecorded nits are fixed — n1 the Daemon Manual's suggestions row (`observed_at` is null for the assistant's suggestion), n2 03's error-code list and rev 7.4's "no contract changed" and its LQ14 place, n3 the LQ16 row's figures and their source, n4 `30689e20` in the status line and rev 3.33's range.
- **rev 3.34 (2026-09-27), the L8 product slice's repair round 1 (`f4a1e5e6..76127cbf` and the documentation commit that records this entry).** Three reviews of rev 3.33 — tests, guarantees, consistency — raised 17 findings: 3 major, 11 minor, 3 nits. Each is fixed and none is rejected; one minor is answered by recording rather than code (the Memory view's origin label). *(Corrected in rev 3.35: they raised 21 — 3 major, 11 minor, 7 nits. Consistency's nits n1–n4 were neither fixed nor recorded here; rev 3.35 fixes them. And more than one minor was answered by recording: besides the origin label, what stays direct (consistency m1) and the edit of a learned note that sets a saved key (guarantees).)* Behavioural fixes test first (red on rev 3.33's code: 9 failed), and every pin is shown to bite under a negative control. §2.3 step 4, §2.4 rule 1, §3.1 row 5, §3.5, §4.2 rule 5 and its closing paragraph, §4.4 and §4.5 are amended in place; rev 3.33's entry carries three inline corrections; 03 is rev 7.5.
  - **LQ13, the reading tightened** (`f4a1e5e6`; guarantees, major). Rev 3.33 could take a value that the model's own final answer rejected, and could use up a window without a retry. Five shapes did it:
    - a draft before a final `[]`;
    - a draft in reasoning that a `</think>` in mid-answer closed;
    - a quoted `{"items": …}` of non-items ahead of a final non-items object;
    - an echoed transcript row `[{"n": 1, …}]`, taken as an items list whose every entry was then dropped, so the cursor advanced with nothing learned;
    - a draft behind a `<think>` that never closed.

    The rule now (`task_extraction::read_json_answer`, §2.4 rule 1):
    - the whole answer is read as JSON first;
    - else the answer is the text after the last `</think>`, or, when a `</think>` ends the answer, the text before it, read whole only;
    - a `<think>` that never closes is no answer;
    - a value found in prose, and a bare array anywhere, counts as the items only when every entry carries a string `content`;
    - so `[]` means "nothing to keep", and a final `[]` outranks any draft. Rev 3.33 pinned a bare `[]` as unusable; it is now zero items.

    Task-output extraction holds a value found in prose to `{"extractions": [...]}` with `content` entries in the same way. The six cases are tests, and so is an echoed row held as an unusable answer through a flush (the window holds).

    **For the lead:** a value found in prose is still the *last one of the expected shape*, as the spec asked. So a complete quoted `{"items": [...]}` followed by a final non-items object is still taken. Whether prose should yield only its last value, which would close that case too, is the lead's call.
  - **The cut rule, pinned for summaries and runs** (`f4a1e5e6`; tests, minor). A summary cut at `max_tokens` in the prose after a complete object is reported "cut at max_tokens (N)" and writes nothing. A run's answer cut at `max_tokens`, with its `extractions` object in prose, is a JSON parse failure and stores nothing. Each test fails with its cut rule taken out (the reviews' NC-A, NC-B).
  - **The scan's unclosed bracket** (`f4a1e5e6`; tests, nit). Rev 3.33's doc (and `6120812b`'s message) said nothing is dug out of a bracketed stretch that is not JSON. One whose bracket never closes is skipped by itself instead. So a stray `[` in prose does not hide the answer after it, and a value nested in a malformed wrapper that never closes is then found — the two cannot be told apart. The docs now say so, and tests pin both cases and a closed malformed wrapper, which is skipped whole.
  - **LQ11** (`edb62bbb`):
    - **An exact duplicate of a saved note is that note** (tests, minor). The file layer answers `dedup: existing` for a suggestion that would replace an active note with an identical one — kind, title, body, key, aliases, tags. Rev 3.33 made the probe's `Laptop` a "replace X with X" suggestion.
    - **The note a store meets under its key** (guarantees, minor). This is the R9 winner of the partition the store would land in; R9 is per partition.
      - With no `supersedes`, it is the first *saved* winner in the partition `scope` names, else in the project's and then the home one. The store then goes to that note's partition.
      - A `supersedes` that names a note which is not saved names instead the saved R9 winner of the store's key in that note's partition. This covers a learned same-key loser, and the chat model's own earlier suggestion.
      - Rev 3.33 stopped at the first partition holding *any* winner. So a learned note in the project hid a note saved at home, and the tool's note landed at home, silently outranked by the saved note — or, for an `external` or `imported` one, outranking it.
      - Left as recorded residue (§3.1 row 5, 03 §4): a `memory_update` edit of a learned note that sets a saved note's key.
    - **`memory_store`'s result for a suggestion carries `superseded: []`** (consistency, minor). `replaces` names the note, as `memory_update`'s result already did. The service's `StoreOutput.superseded` stays the frontmatter's link.
    - **The in-chat card and the `<memory_suggestions>` line** (guarantees minor, consistency major) say "suggested by the assistant on <date>" for the chat model's suggestion, told apart by LQ14's datum (`in_chat::by_the_assistant`). §4.2 rule 5's premise is restated: the text a card shows is its writer's. Test helpers that model learning's suggestions now set `observed_at`, as the extractor does.
    - Not built, since the finding named it optional: refusing `memory_confirm` for a suggestion not listed on the turn. The Approve on the card is still required either way, and §4.2 rule 5 records this.
